satisfies these vanishing conditions too. Spelling out the property in (
ii
) with
\tilde{\kappa}=-1
, it follows that there is a
\rho
such that

E^{1}_{p^{N}n_{i},p^{N}d_{i}-1,p^{N}f_{i}-r}=\pi_{p^{N}n_{i},p^{N}d_{i}-1,p^{N}f_{i}-r}(C\tau\otimes\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1})))

vanishes as long as
p^{N}f_{i}-r<\tfrac{f_{i}}{d_{i}}(p^{N}d_{i}-1)+\rho
. In particular it vanishes for all

r\geq r_{0}:=\tfrac{f_{i}}{d_{i}}-\rho+1>p^{N}f_{i}-\tfrac{f_{i}}{d_{i}}(p^{N}d_{i}-1)-\rho

independently of
N
.
∎

We make use of these vanishing estimates as follows. The unit map

u:\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1}))

lands in the centre, and hence gives a map of spectral sequences of algebras landing in the centre (of each page), which on the
E^{1}
-page has the form

(C\tau\otimes u)_{*}:W_{1}((-1)_{*}H_{*,*}^{E_{2}}(\mathbb{I}))=\pi_{*,*,*}(C\tau\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow E^{1}_{*,*,*}.

In particular we have a class
(C\tau\otimes u)_{*}(x_{i})\in E^{1}_{n_{i},d_{i},f_{i}}
lying in the centre.

We claim that some power
(C\tau\otimes u)_{*}(x_{i})^{p^{M_{i}}}
is a permanent cycle in the spectral sequence
\{E^{r}_{*,*,*}\}_{r}
. As it comes from a filtered
E_{1}
-algebra, this spectral sequence is one of associative rings, i.e. the differentials satisfy the Leibniz rule. Thus if a class
z\in E^{r}_{*,*,*}
lies in the centre then
d^{r}(z^{p})=pz^{p-1}d^{r}(z)=0
so
z^{p}
survives until
E^{r+1}_{*,*,*}
. This may be used to find
p
-th powers of
(C\tau\otimes u)_{*}(x_{i})
which survive until arbitrarily late pages of the spectral sequence. In particular some
(C\tau\otimes u)_{*}(x_{i})^{p^{M_{i}}}
survives until
E^{r_{0}}_{p^{M_{i}}n_{i},p^{M_{i}}d_{i},p^{M_{i}}f_{i}}
for the
r_{0}
given by Lemma
5.7
(
iii
). By that Lemma we have
E^{r_{0}}_{p^{M_{i}}n_{i},p^{M_{i}}d_{i}-1,p^{M_{i}}f_{i}-r_{0}}=0
and so
d^{r_{0}}((C\tau\otimes u)_{*}(x_{i})^{p^{M_{i}}})=0
and
(C\tau\otimes u)_{*}(x_{i})^{p^{M_{i}}}
survives to
E^{r_{0}+1}_{p^{M_{i}}n_{i},p^{M_{i}}d_{i},p^{M_{i}}f_{i}}
. But the conclusion of Lemma
5.7
(
iii
) holds for all
r\geq r_{0}
, so the class
(C\tau\otimes u)_{*}(x_{i})^{p^{M_{i}}}
continues to be a cycle on all later pages, and survives to
(C\tau\otimes u)_{*}(x_{i})^{p^{M_{i}}}\in E^{\infty}_{p^{M_{i}}n_{i},p^{M_{i}}d_{i},p^{M_{i}}f_{i}}
. It detects a filtered map

\phi_{i}:S^{p^{M_{i}}n_{i},p^{M_{i}}d_{i},p^{M_{i}}f_{i}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/({\phi}_{1},\ldots,{\phi}_{i-1})),

which by construction is an
x_{i}
self-map.

This establishes part (
i
) of Theorem
5.6
. By raising
\phi_{i}
to further
p
-th powers, we may assume that
M_{i}\geq M
, which establishes part (
iii
). For part (
ii
) we observe that Lemma
5.7
(
i
) gives that there is a
\kappa
such that
\pi_{n,d,f}(C\tau\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/({\phi}_{1},\ldots,{\phi}_{i}))=0
for
d<\tfrac{d_{i+1}}{n_{i+1}}n+\kappa
, so running the spectral sequence for the filtered object
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/({\phi}_{1},\ldots,{\phi}_{i})
shows that
\pi_{n,d}(\tau^{-1}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/({\phi}_{1},\ldots,{\phi}_{i}))=0
for
d<\tfrac{d_{i+1}}{n_{i+1}}n+\kappa
too. This finishes the proof of Theorem
5.6
.

Remark 5.8
.

Rather than relying on centrality of
(C\tau\otimes u)_{*}(x_{i})
and the Leibniz rule in the spectral sequence
\{E^{r}_{*,*,*}\}_{r}
associated to the filtered
E_{1}
-algebra
\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1}))
, we could instead proceed as follows. In a filtered
E_{2}
-algebra such as
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
, if
y\in E^{r}_{*,*,*}
then an element
Q^{s}(y)
obtained by applying a Dyer–Lashof operation survives until
E^{pr}_{*,*,*}
(and
d^{pr}(Q^{s}(y))
is represented by
Q^{s}(d^{r}(y))
), see
[
14
, Theorem 16.8]
. In particular,
x_{i}\in E^{1}_{n_{i},d_{i},f_{i}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R})
so
x_{i}^{p}=Q^{s}(x_{i})
(with
s=d_{i}
if
p=2
and
s=d_{i}/2
if
p
is odd) survives until
E^{p}_{*,*,*}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R})
, and by the same principle
(C\tau\otimes u)_{*}(x_{i}^{p^{M_{i}}})
survives until
E^{p^{M_{i}}}_{*,*,*}
. The (only) advantage of this over the Leibniz rule method is that a lower power of
(C\tau\otimes u)_{*}(x_{i})
needs to be taken for it to survive to a given page.

5.4.
E_{k}
-algebras,
k\geq 3

If
\mathbb{R}
is an augmented
E_{k}
-algebra with
k\geq 3
, then we can run the discussion and proofs of this section using the filtered
E_{k}
-algebra
\mathrm{fil}_{*}^{E_{k}}\mathbb{R}
instead, which has associated graded

C\tau\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}^{E_{k}}\mathbb{R}\simeq\mathbb{E}_{k}((-1)_{*}Q^{E_{k}^{\text{nu}}}\mathbb{I}).

It is still the case that (SCE) implies that
H^{E_{k}}_{n,d}(\mathbb{I})=0
for
d<n-1
. The work of F. Cohen in this case describes the homotopy groups of this object as the free
W_{k-1}
-algebra on the trigraded vector space
(-1)_{*}H^{E_{k}}_{*,*}(\mathbb{I})
, and it is again a free graded-commutative
\mathbbm{k}
-algebra. But now the Browder bracket or
\xi
or
\zeta
applied to elements of
H^{E_{k}}_{*,*}(\mathbb{I})
end up in tridegrees
\pi_{n,d,f}(C\tau\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}^{E_{k}}\mathbb{R})
with
d\geq n
, and this improves further with repeated applications. The multiplicative generators below the diagonal are therefore just those admissible iterated Dyer–Lashof operations applied to elements of
\bigoplus_{n\geq 1}(-1)_{*}H^{E_{k}}_{n,n-1}(\mathbb{I})
which happen to lie below the diagonal. We record this as follows, and omit the proof which is elementary but laborious.

Lemma 5.9
.

If
p
is even, then the multiplicative generators below the diagonal are the elements
Q^{2^{r-1}n}\cdots Q^{2n}Q^{n}(x)
with
x\in H^{E_{k}}_{n,n-1}(\mathbb{I})
.

If
p
is odd, then the multiplicative generators below the diagonal are the elements
\beta^{\epsilon}Q^{p^{r-1}n/2}\cdots Q^{pn/2}Q^{n/2}(x)
with
x\in H^{E_{k}}_{n,n-1}(\mathbb{I})
(implicitly
n
is even if
r>0
).∎

This describes a basis for
V_{*,*,*}
. If
p
is even then this is
X_{*,*,*}
. If
p
is odd then
X_{*,*,*}
is spanned by the elements we have described which have even homological degree (i.e. the
x
’s with
n
odd, and the
\beta Q^{p^{r-1}n/2}\cdots Q^{pn/2}Q^{n/2}(x)
’s with
n
even). In either case we may take these elements, appropriately ordered, as the
x_{i}
.

6.
Efficient Smith–Toda complexes

The argument presented in Section
5
produces, for a given slope
\lambda<1
, a Smith–Toda complex
\mathbb{R}/(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{r})
with a slope
\lambda
vanishing line, but the endomorphisms
\alpha_{i}
can be nilpotent, or even zero. The corresponding filtered endomorphism
\phi_{i}
of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1})
is never nilpotent, as in the associated graded it is detected by (some power of) the non-nilpotent element
x_{i}
: this is precisely why we had to kill them, in order to force
x_{i}
to become nilpotent.

One feels that if
\alpha_{i}
is nilpotent then it ought not to be necessary to kill it: doing so does not take one out of the thick subcategory one resides in, so cannot (by itself) improve the slope of a vanishing line. In this section we wish to explain how to make Smith–Toda complexes where we only kill those
x_{i}
’s for which the corresponding
\alpha_{i}
’s are non-nilpotent. In fact, we will explain how to do slightly better than this.

We will do this by making more refined arguments in the category of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-modules, inspired by recent work with synthetic spectra. The essential idea is the following. To show that a power of
u_{*}(x_{i})
survives in the spectral sequence for
\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
, for some
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-module
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
, it is enough to know that the vanishing property of Lemma
5.7
(
iii
) holds on
some
page of the spectral sequence for the filtered object
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
, not necessarily on the
E^{1}
-page. This is best managed by considering the filtered homotopy groups of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
(analogous to the synthetic homotopy groups), and working with lines beyond which they are uniformly bounded
\tau
-power torsion. Indeed, a filtered endomorphism
\phi_{i}
yields a nilpotent
\alpha_{i}
precisely when some power of
\phi_{i}
is
\tau
-torsion.

6.1.
y
self-maps

Recall that there is a filtered algebra map

u:\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})

landing in the centre. Generalising Definition
5.5
, we have the following.

Definition 6.1
.

For a
y\in\pi_{N,D,F}(C\tau\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R})
, a filtered map

\psi:S^{p^{M}N,p^{M}D,p^{M}F}\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}

is a
y
self-map
if
C\tau\otimes{\psi}=(C\tau\otimes u)_{*}(y^{p^{M}})
.

Our first goal is to describe a condition which guarantees the existence (and later uniqueness up to nilpotence) of
y
self-maps.

Definition 6.2
.

For
\lambda\geq 0
and
\mu\leq 0
, let
\mathsf{W}(\lambda,\mu)\subseteq\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathsf{mod}
be the full subcategory consisting of the finite
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-modules
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
for which there exists an
r
such that:

(I)

There is a
\kappa
such that
E^{r+1}_{n,d,*}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})=0
for
d<\lambda n+\kappa
.

(II)

For each
\tilde{\kappa}
there is a
\rho
such that
E^{r+1}_{n,d,f}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})=0
whenever
d=\lambda n+\tilde{\kappa}
and
f<\mu d+\rho
.

It will be convenient to also work with the following alternative formulation. The equivalence between them is an elaboration of
[
9
, Proposition 11.6]
, cf.
[
24
]
.

Proposition 6.3
.

These conditions are equivalent to asking that:

(A)

There is a
\kappa
such that every class in
\pi_{n,d,*}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
with
d<\lambda n+\kappa
is
\tau^{r}
-torsion.

(B)

For each
\tilde{\kappa}
there is a
\rho
such that every class in
\pi_{n,d,f}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})

with
d=\lambda n+\tilde{\kappa}
and
f<\mu d+\rho
is
\tau^{r}
-torsion.

Proof.

Tensoring
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
with the cofibre sequence
S^{0,0,1}\overset{\tau}{\to}S^{0,0,0}\to C\tau
and taking homotopy groups gives the exact couple

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{n,d,f-1}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{n,d,f}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{n,d,f}(C\tau\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{1}_{n,d,f}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}

which defines the spectral sequence
\{E^{r}_{*,*,*}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})\}_{r}
. We explained in Section
2.7.2
that when
\mathbb{R}
satisfies axiom (C), which we are assuming, the filtered object
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
is complete, and therefore the finite
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-module
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
is complete too. Recall that
x\in E^{1}_{n,d,f}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
survives to
E^{r}_{n,d,f}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
if and only if
\partial(x)=\tau^{r-1}\cdot y
for some
y\in\pi_{n,d,f-r}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
, in which case
d^{r}([x])=[q(y)]
.

The argument is deliberately repetitive: the equivalence (II)
\Leftrightarrow
(B) is an elaboration of the equivalence (I)
\Leftrightarrow
(A), and for clarity we give both in detail.

Suppose (I) holds with parameter
\kappa
, and let
x\in\pi_{n,d,f}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
with
d<\lambda n+\kappa
. Then
q(x)\in E^{1}_{n,d,f}
is a permanent cycle, but as
E^{r+1}_{n,d,f}=0
there must be a differential
d^{s}([u])=[q(x)]
for some
s\leq r
. That is,
\partial(u)=\tau^{s-1}\cdot z
and
q(z)=q(x)
. Then
x-z=\tau\cdot x^{\prime}
, and as
\tau\cdot\partial(-)=0
it follows that
0=\tau^{s}(x-\tau\cdot x^{\prime})
, so multiplying by
\tau^{r-s}
gives
\tau^{r}\cdot x=\tau^{r+1}\cdot x^{\prime}
. The same reasoning applies to
x^{\prime}
, showing that
\tau^{r}\cdot x=\tau^{r+2}\cdot x^{\prime\prime}
, and so on: it follows that
\tau^{r}\cdot x
is infinitely divisible by
\tau
, so it lifts to an element of the limit
\lim_{p}\pi_{n,d,f+r-p}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
. But the Milnor sequence

0\to{\lim_{p}}^{1}\pi_{n,d+1,f+r-p}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})\to\pi_{n,d}(\lim\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})\to\lim_{p}\pi_{n,d,f+r-p}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})\to 0

and the fact that
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
is complete, i.e. that
\lim\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\simeq 0
, means that this limit is zero, and so
\tau^{r}\cdot x=0
. Thus (A) holds with parameter
\kappa
.

Conversely, suppose (A) holds with parameter
\kappa
, and let
[u]\in E^{r+1}_{n,d,f}
with
d<\lambda n+\kappa
. As
u\in E^{1}_{n,d,f}
survives until
E^{r+1}
, we have
\partial(u)=\tau^{r}\cdot z
for some
z\in\pi_{n,d-1,f-1-r}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
. Now
d-1<\lambda n+\kappa
too so by (A) we have
\tau^{r}\cdot z=0
and hence
\partial(u)=0
, meaning that
u=q(x)
for
x\in\pi_{n,d,f}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
. As
d<\lambda n+\kappa
we have
\tau^{r}\cdot x=0
. Let
s\leq r
be such that
\tau^{s-1}\cdot x\neq 0
but
\tau^{s}\cdot x=0
. Then
\tau^{s-1}\cdot x=\partial(v)
, meaning that
d^{s}([v])=[q(x)]=[u]
, so that
[u]=0\in E^{r+1}_{n,d,f}
. Thus (I) holds with parameter
\kappa
.

Now suppose (II) holds and choose a
\tilde{\kappa}
, and let
x\in\pi_{n,d,f}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
with
d=\lambda n+\tilde{\kappa}
. Then
q(x)\in E^{1}_{n,d,f}
is a permanent cycle. Applying (II) with parameter
\tilde{\kappa}
we find that there is a
\rho
such that
E^{r+1}_{n,d,f}=0
as long as
f<\mu d+\rho
. Supposing then that this is the case, we must have a differential
d^{s}([u])=[q(x)]
for some
s\leq r
, so
\partial(u)=\tau^{s-1}\cdot z
with
q(z)=q(x)
, and as above we deduce that
\tau^{r}\cdot x=\tau^{r+1}\cdot x^{\prime}
. This
x^{\prime}
has the same grading and homological degree, but smaller filtration than
x
, so the above reasoning continues to apply to it: it follows that
\tau^{r}\cdot x
is infinitely divisible by
\tau
, so as above it vanishes. We have shown that any
x\in\pi_{n,d,f}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
with
d=\lambda n+\tilde{\kappa}
and
f<\mu d+\rho
is
\tau^{r}
-torsion, so (B) holds.

Conversely, suppose (B) holds and choose a
\tilde{\kappa}
, and let
[u]\in E^{r+1}_{n,d,f}
with
d=\lambda n+\tilde{\kappa}
. We have
\partial(u)=\tau^{r}\cdot z
for some
z\in\pi_{n,d-1,f-1-r}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
. Applying (B) with parameter
\tilde{\kappa}^{\prime}:=\tilde{\kappa}-1
implies that there is a
\rho^{\prime}
such that
\pi_{n,d-1,f-1-r}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
is
\tau^{r}
-torsion as long as
f-1-r<\mu(d-1)+\rho^{\prime}
, i.e. for
f<\mu d+(\rho^{\prime}-\mu+1+r)
. Supposing then that this is the case, we have
\partial(u)=\tau^{r}\cdot z=0
and so
u=q(x)
with
x\in\pi_{n,d,f}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
. Applying (B) again with parameter
\tilde{\kappa}^{\prime\prime}:=\tilde{\kappa}
implies that there is a
\rho^{\prime\prime}
such that
\pi_{n,d,f}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
is
\tau^{r}
-torsion as long as
f<\mu d+\rho^{\prime\prime}
. Supposing that this is also the case, there is a
s\leq r
with
\tau^{s-1}\cdot x\neq 0
and
\tau^{s}\cdot x=0
, meaning that there is a
v
with
\partial(v)=\tau^{s-1}\cdot x
, and hence a differential
d^{s}([v])=[q(x)]=[u]
, so that
[u]=0\in E^{r+1}_{n,d,f}
. We have shown that any
[u]\in E^{r+1}_{n,d,f}
with
d=\lambda n+\tilde{\kappa}
and
f<\mu d+\min\{\rho^{\prime}-\mu+1+r,\rho^{\prime\prime}\}
vanishes, so (II) holds.
∎

Definition 6.4
.

Let
\mathsf{T}\subseteq\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathsf{mod}
be the full subcategory consisting of the finite
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-modules
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
such that
\{\pi_{n,d,*}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})\}_{n,d}
are uniformly bounded
\tau
-power torsion (i.e. are all annihilated by
\tau^{r}
for some fixed
r
)

Proposition 6.5
.

(i)

\mathsf{W}(\lambda,\mu)
is a thick subcategory.

(ii)

We have
\mathsf{W}(\lambda,\mu)\subseteq\mathsf{W}(\bar{\lambda},\bar{\mu})
if
\lambda>\bar{\lambda}
, or if
\lambda=\bar{\lambda}
and
\mu\geq\bar{\mu}
.

(iii)

\mathsf{T}\subseteq\mathsf{W}(\lambda,\mu)
for any
\lambda
and
\mu
.

Proof.

For (
i
), first note that objects of
\mathsf{W}(\lambda,\mu)
are clearly closed under retracts, and under shifts by any
S^{n,d,f}
. Let
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}\to\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\to\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}
be a cofibre sequence where the outer terms are in
\mathsf{W}(\lambda,\mu)
. To verify (A) for
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
, note that there are
r^{\prime}
,
\kappa^{\prime}
,
r^{\prime\prime}
and
\kappa^{\prime\prime}
such that
\pi_{n,d,*}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime})
is
\tau^{r^{\prime}}
-torsion for
d<\lambda n+\kappa^{\prime}
and
\pi_{n,d,*}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime})
is
\tau^{r^{\prime\prime}}
-torsion for
d<\lambda n+\kappa^{\prime\prime}
. From the long exact sequence it follows that
\pi_{n,d,*}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
is
\tau^{r^{\prime}+r^{\prime\prime}}
-torsion for
d<\lambda n+\min\{\kappa^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime\prime}\}
, verifying (A) with
r:=r^{\prime}+r^{\prime\prime}
. To verify (B) for
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
, let
\tilde{\kappa}
be given. Then there are
\rho^{\prime}
and
\rho^{\prime\prime}
such that the filtered homotopy groups of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
are
\tau^{r^{\prime}}
-torsion for
d=\lambda n+\tilde{\kappa}
and
f<\mu d+\rho^{\prime}
, and those of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}
are
\tau^{r^{\prime\prime}}
-torsion for
d=\lambda n+\tilde{\kappa}
and
f<\mu d+\rho^{\prime\prime}
, then the long exact sequence shows that those of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
are
\tau^{r^{\prime}+r^{\prime\prime}}
-torsion for
d=\lambda n+\tilde{\kappa}
and
f<\mu d+\min\{\rho^{\prime},\rho^{\prime\prime}\}
. This verifies (B) with
r=r^{\prime}+r^{\prime\prime}
too.

For (
ii
), first note that as
\bar{\lambda}\leq\lambda
, and the homotopy groups
\pi_{n,d,*}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
vanish for
n\ll 0
or
d\ll 0
by Axiom (C) and the fact that
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
is a finite
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-module, property (A) for
\bar{\lambda}
follows from that for
\lambda
. For property (B), there are two cases.

If
\lambda=\bar{\lambda}
then along each line
d=\lambda n+\tilde{\kappa}
we know that there is a
\rho
such that
\pi_{n,d,f}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
is
\tau^{r}
-torsion for
f<\mu d+\rho
. Using again that these homotopy groups vanish for
d\ll 0
and the fact that
\bar{\mu}\leq\mu
, it follows that they are also
\tau^{r}
-torsion for
f<\bar{\mu}d+\rho^{\prime}
for some
\rho^{\prime}
.

If instead
\lambda>\bar{\lambda}
then by (A) each line
d=\bar{\lambda}n+\tilde{\kappa}
only contains finitely-many
\pi_{n,d,*}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
’s which are not
\tau^{r}
-torsion. As these are finitely-generated
\mathbbm{k}[\tau]
-modules, each of them vanishes in small enough filtration. Thus
\rho
may be chosen sufficiently small so that all these finitely-many
\mathbbm{k}[\tau]
-modules vanish in filtration
f<\rho
, and hence also for
f<\bar{\mu}d+\rho
(as
\bar{\mu}\leq 0
). Having done this, it follows that
\pi_{n,d,f}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
is
\tau^{r}
-torsion for
d=\bar{\lambda}n+\tilde{\kappa}
and
f<\bar{\mu}d+\rho
.

Item (
iii
) is immediate from (A) and (B).
∎

Theorem 6.6
.

Let
y\in\pi_{N,D,F}(C\tau\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R})
be given. Then all objects of
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
admit a
y
self-map.

Proof.

Let
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\in\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
. As it is a finite
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-module its endomorphism object
\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
and so is also in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
. By (II) applied with
\tilde{\kappa}=-1
there is an
r
and a
\rho
such that

(6.1)

E^{r+2}_{p^{M}N,p^{M}D-1,p^{M}F-s}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}))=0

for
-s<\rho
and all
M
. Set
M_{0}:=\max\{r+2,1-\rho\}
.

The central class

u_{*}(y^{p^{M_{0}}})\in E^{1}_{p^{M_{0}}N,p^{M_{0}}D,p^{M_{0}}F}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}))

survives until
E^{{M_{0}}}_{p^{M_{0}}N,p^{M_{0}}D,p^{M_{0}}F}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}))
, by applications of the Leibniz rule. For each
s\geq{M_{0}}
the class

d^{s}(u_{*}(y^{p^{M_{0}}}))\in E^{s}_{p^{M_{0}}N,p^{M_{0}}D-1,p^{M_{0}}F-s}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}))

must vanish by (
6.1
). Thus
u_{*}(y^{p^{M_{0}}})
is a permanent cycle, and detects a filtered homotopy class
\psi\in\pi_{p^{M_{0}}N,p^{M_{0}}D,p^{M_{0}}F}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}))
which is a
y
self-map.
∎

Uniqueness up to nilpotence and several other useful properties of
y
self-maps are collected as follows.

Theorem 6.7
.

Let
y\in\pi_{N,D,F}(C\tau\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R})
be given.

(i)

If
\psi
is a
y
self-map of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\in\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
, then after perhaps taking a
p
-th power it lies in the (graded) centre of
\pi_{*,*,*}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}))
.

(ii)

If
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
are
y
self-maps of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\in\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
, then they agree after perhaps taking
p
-th powers.

(iii)

If
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
and
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N}
are in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
and have
y
self-maps
\psi^{\mathbb{M}}
and
\psi^{\mathbb{N}}
of the same tridegrees, then after perhaps taking
p
-th powers they intertwine all
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-module maps
f:S^{n,d,f}\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\to\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N}
.

(iv)

If
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\overset{f}{\to}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N}\overset{g}{\to}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{P}\overset{\partial}{\to}\Sigma\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
is a distinguished triangle of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-modules, and
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
and
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N}
are in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
, then so is
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{P}
. Furthermore they admit
y
self-maps
\psi^{\mathbb{M}}
,
\psi^{\mathbb{N}}
, and
\psi^{\mathbb{P}}
which assemble into a morphism of distinguished triangles.

Proof.

For (
i
) we follow the idea of
[
25
, Lemma 3.5]
, though expressing ourselves in terms of filtered objects and their filtered homotopy groups rather than the associated spectral sequence. Consider the commuting endomorphisms

\psi\circ(-),(-)\circ\psi:S^{p^{M}N,p^{M}D,p^{M}F}\otimes\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})\longrightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}).

On associated graded they are given by post- and pre-composition with
u_{*}(y^{p^{M}})
, which lies in the graded centre of
C\tau\otimes\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
, so they are homotopic up to a sign of
(-1)^{p^{M}D}=(-1)^{D}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}\subseteq\mathbbm{k}^{\times}
. Thus the (signed) difference may be divided by
\tau
to give a filtered map

[\psi\circ(-)-(-1)^{D}(-)\circ\psi]:S^{p^{M}N,p^{M}D,p^{M}F-1}\otimes\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})\longrightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})

of filtration 1 less (which is no longer a difference of two maps). Consider its
p^{L}
-th power

[\psi\circ(-)-(-1)^{D}(-)\circ\psi]^{p^{L}}:S^{p^{M+L}N,p^{M+L}D,p^{M+L}F-p^{L}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})),

for some
L
to be chosen shortly.

The endomorphism object
\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
is again in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
by Proposition
6.5
(i), so
\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}))
is too by the same reasoning. Thus by property (B) there is an
r
and a
\rho
such that

\pi_{n,\tfrac{D}{N}n,f}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})))\text{ is $\tau^{r}$-torsion for }f<\tfrac{F}{N}n+\rho\text{ and all }n.

If we choose
L
large enough that
-p^{L}<\rho
then it follows that

[\psi\circ(-)-(-1)^{D}(-)\circ\psi]^{p^{L}}\in\pi_{p^{M+L}N,p^{M+L}D,p^{M+L}F-p^{L}}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})))

is
\tau^{r}
-torsion, and if in addition
L
is large enough that
p^{L}\geq r
then multiplying by
\tau^{p^{L}}
gives

(\psi\circ(-)-(-1)^{D}(-)\circ\psi)^{p^{L}}=0\in\pi_{p^{M+L}N,p^{M+L}D,p^{M+L}F}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}))).

As the endomorphisms
\psi\circ(-)
and
(-)\circ\psi
of
\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
commute, the binomial expansion shows that
(\psi\circ(-)-(-1)^{D}(-)\circ\psi)^{p^{L}}=\psi^{p^{L}}\circ(-)-(-1)^{D}(-)\circ\psi^{p^{L}}
, or in other words that
\psi^{p^{L}}\circ(-)\simeq(-1)^{p^{M+L}D}(-)\circ\psi^{p^{L}}
as endomorphisms of
\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
. In particular
\psi^{p^{L}}
lies in the graded centre of
\pi_{*,*,*}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}))
.

For (
ii
) the argument is highly similar. After raising
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
to perhaps different
p
-th powers, we may suppose they have the same tridegree, say
(p^{M}N,p^{M}D,p^{M}F)
, and by (
i
) are both central in
\pi_{*,*,*}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}))
. Then the filtered map

\psi^{\prime}-\psi:S^{p^{M}N,p^{M}D,p^{M}F}\longrightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})

is given by
u_{*}(y^{p^{M}})-u_{*}(y^{p^{M}})=0
on associated graded, so it may be divided by
\tau
to give a map

[\psi^{\prime}-\psi]:S^{p^{M}N,p^{M}D,p^{M}F-1}\longrightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})

of filtration 1 less (which is no longer a difference of two maps). Thus its
p^{L}
-th power is

[\psi^{\prime}-\psi]^{p^{L}}:S^{p^{M+L}N,p^{M+L}D,p^{M+L}F-p^{L}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}).

Again,
\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
is in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
so by (B) there is an
r
and a
\rho
such that

\pi_{n,\tfrac{D}{N}n,f}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}))\text{ is $\tau^{r}$-torsion for }f<\tfrac{F}{N}n+\rho\text{ and all }n.

Choosing
L
large enough that
-p^{L}<\rho
and
p^{L}\geq r
, it follows that
[\psi^{\prime}-\psi]^{p^{L}}
is
\tau^{r}
-torsion and so is
\tau^{p^{L}}
-torsion, and hence that
(\psi^{\prime}-\psi)^{p^{L}}=0
. As
\psi
is central we can expand this binomially to obtain
(\psi^{\prime})^{p^{L}}=(\psi)^{p^{L}}\in\pi_{*,*,*}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}))
.

For (
iii
), we consider the
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-module
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M},\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N})
. As
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
is a finite
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-module and
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N}
is in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
,
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M},\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N})
is also in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
. Suppose that
\psi^{\mathbb{M}}
and
\psi^{\mathbb{N}}
both have tridegree
(p^{M}N,p^{M}D,p^{M}F)
. Then the maps

(-)\circ\psi^{\mathbb{M}}:S^{p^{M}N,p^{M}D,p^{M}F}\otimes\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M},\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M},\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N})

\psi^{\mathbb{N}}\circ(-):S^{p^{M}N,p^{M}D,p^{M}F}\otimes\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M},\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M},\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N})

are both
y
self-maps, so by part (
ii
) they agree after perhaps taking further
p
-th powers. In particular there is a fixed
p^{L}
such that
f\circ(\psi^{\mathbb{M}})^{p^{L}}\simeq(\psi^{\mathbb{N}})^{p^{L}}\circ f
for all
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-module maps
f:S^{n,d,f}\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\to\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N}
.

For (
iv
), we have already explained that
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
is a thick subcategory, so
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{P}
is in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
. Choosing any
y
self-maps for all three objects, of the same tridegree, after raising them to a suitable
p
-th power it follows from part (
iii
) that they commute with
f
,
g
, and
\partial
, so give a morphism of distinguished triangles.
∎

6.2.
Nilpotence up to
\tau
-power torsion

The following lemma formalises how when working modulo objects of
\mathsf{T}
, i.e. finite
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-modules whose homotopy groups are uniformly bounded
\tau
-power torsion, we may treat endomorphism which are nilpotent up to
\tau
-torsion as though they were nilpotent. We then give some applications of this.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
f
be a self-map of a finite
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-module
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
such that some iterate of
f
is
\tau
-power torsion. Then
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}/f
and
\mathsf{T}
.

In particular, if
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
is finite and
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}/f
is in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
then so is
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\tau^{r}f^{K}\simeq 0
. There is a cofibre sequence

S^{0,0,r}\otimes C\tau^{r}\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}/(\tau^{r}f^{K})\longrightarrow\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}/(f^{K})

upon which we make the following observations:

(a)

The left term is a finite
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-module with uniformly bounded
\tau
-power torsion homotopy groups (as they are
\tau^{2r}
-torsion).

(b)

The middle term contains
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
as a retract (as
\tau^{r}f^{K}
is null).

(c)

The right term lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}/f
(by Lemma
2.5
(
ii
)).

The claim follows.
∎

Lemma 6.9
.

Fix slopes
(\lambda,\mu)
.

(i)

Let
x_{s},\ldots,x_{t}
be those
x
’s having slopes
(\lambda,\mu)
. If
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{s-1})
is any filtered Smith–Toda complex obtained by taking the iterated cofibre of a sequence of
x_{k}
self-maps
\phi_{k}
, then
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{s-1})
lies in
\mathsf{W}(\lambda,\mu)
, and

(ii)

Let
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{r})
be any filtered Smith–Toda complex which lies in
\mathsf{W}(\lambda,\mu)
, and such that each
\psi_{i}
has slopes
<(\lambda,\mu)
. Then
\mathsf{W}(\lambda,\mu)
is generated as a thick subcategory by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{r})
and
\mathsf{T}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.7
(
i
) there is a
\kappa
such that
\pi_{n,d,f}(C\tau\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{s-1}))=0
for
d<\lambda n+\kappa
, and by Lemma
5.7
(
ii
) for each
\tilde{\kappa}
there is a
\rho
such that
\pi_{n,d,f}(C\tau\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{s-1}))=0
for
d=\lambda n+\tilde{\kappa}
and
f<\mu d+\rho
. In particular it satisfies (I) and (II) of Definition
6.2
with
r=0
, so
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{s-1})\in\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
.

To prove (
ii
), suppose
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\in\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
. We observe that for each
1\leq k\leq r
the endomorphism
\mathrm{Id}\otimes\psi_{k}
of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{k-1})
has an iterate which is
\tau
-power torsion, because this object lies in
\mathsf{W}(\lambda,\mu)
and our assumption on the slopes of
\psi_{k}
. By many applications of Lemma
6.8
it follows that
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
is in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{k})
and
\mathsf{T}
. As
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{k})
is in particular in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{k})
, the claim follows.
∎

Definition 6.10
.

Let
y\in\pi_{N,D,F}(C\tau\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R})
be given. Write
\mathsf{N}(y)\subset\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
for the full subcategory of those objects such that each
y
self-map (which exists by Theorem
6.6
and is unique up to taking powers by Theorem
6.7
(
ii
)) has an iterate which is
\tau
-power torsion.

Lemma 6.11
.

(i)

\mathsf{N}(y)
is a thick subcategory.

(ii)

\mathsf{T}\subseteq\mathsf{N}(y)
for any
y
.

(iii)

If
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\in\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
and
\psi
is a
y
self-map, then
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}/\psi\in\mathsf{N}(y)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}\overset{i}{\to}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\overset{q}{\to}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}
be a cofibre sequence in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
, so all three terms admit
y
self-maps
\psi^{\prime}
,
\psi
, and
\psi^{\prime\prime}
by Theorem
6.6
. Assume the outer terms are in
\mathsf{N}(y)
. By Theorem
6.7
(
iv
) these self-maps may be assumed to have the same tridegree and to commute with the maps
i
and
q
. Raising to appropriate powers, the maps
\psi^{\prime}
and
\psi^{\prime\prime}
may be assumed to be
\tau^{r}
-torsion, giving a map of cofibre sequences of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{p^{M}N,p^{M}D,p^{M}F+r}\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{p^{M}N,p^{M}D,p^{M}F+r}\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{p^{M}N,p^{M}D,p^{M}F+r}\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{r}\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

and so a factorisation

S^{p^{M}N,p^{M}D,p^{M}F+r}\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\overset{q}{\longrightarrow}S^{p^{M}N,p^{M}D,p^{M}F+r}\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}\overset{\text{induced}}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}\overset{i}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}

of
\tau^{r}\psi
. This implies that
(\tau^{r}\psi)^{2}\simeq 0
, as
q\circ i\simeq 0
, so
\psi^{2}
is
\tau^{2r}
-torsion. Finally, any
y
self-map of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
agrees with
\psi
up to taking iterates, by Theorem
6.7
(
ii
), so the analogous conclusion holds for all of them.

It is clear that
\mathsf{N}(y)
is closed under shifts, so to show that it is a thick subcategory it remains to show it is closed under retracts. If
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}\oplus\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}
is in
\mathsf{N}(y)
, then each summand is in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
, so has a
y
self-map. Raising these to appropriate powers we may suppose they have the same degree, hence giving
\psi^{\prime}\oplus\psi^{\prime\prime}
a
y
self-map of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}\oplus\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}
. This has some iterate which is
\tau
-power torsion, from which it follows that both
\psi^{\prime}
and
\psi^{\prime\prime}
do too.

To prove (
ii
), first note that by Proposition
6.5
(
iii
)
\mathsf{T}\subseteq\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D}{N},\tfrac{F}{D})
. As
\mathsf{T}
is a thick subcategory of finite
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-modules, if
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\in\mathsf{T}
then
\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})\in\mathsf{T}
too. So every endomorphism of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
is
\tau
-power torsion, in particular
y
self-maps are.

To prove (
iii
), note that

C\tau\otimes(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}/\psi)\simeq(C\tau\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})/(y^{p^{M}})
on which
y
acts nilpotently. Thus the zero endomorphism of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}/\psi
is a
y
self-map, and hence by Theorem
6.7
(
ii
) any
y
self-map of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}/\psi
is nilpotent.
∎

6.3.
Omitting nilpotent endomorphisms

As a first step towards efficiency of Smith–Toda complexes, we make a slight improvement to Theorem
5.6
. Let us say that an
x_{i}
is
redundant
if the
x_{i}
self-map

\phi_{i}:S^{p^{M_{i}}n_{i},p^{M_{i}}d_{i},p^{M_{i}}f_{i}}\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1})\longrightarrow\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1})

is nilpotent after inverting
\tau
, i.e. has an iterate which is
\tau
-power torsion. Equivalently,
(\tau\phi_{i})^{-1}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1})\simeq 0
. The latter formulation makes it clear that this property does not depend on the choices of
x_{j}
self-maps
\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1}
or
\phi_{i}
as follows. If
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\phi^{\prime}_{i-1})
is another filtered Smith–Toda complex with an
x_{i}
self map
\phi^{\prime}_{i}
then by Lemma
6.9
(
ii
) it lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1})
and
\mathsf{T}
. Using that inverting
\tau
kills
\mathsf{T}
, and the uniqueness (Theorem
6.7
(
ii
)) and naturality (Theorem
6.7
(
iii
)) up to taking
p
-th powers of
x_{i}
self-maps, it follows that
(\tau\phi^{\prime}_{i})^{-1}
annihilates
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\phi^{\prime}_{i-1})
too.

Theorem 6.12
.

Let
x_{j_{1}},x_{j_{2}},\ldots,x_{j_{s}}
be the subsequence of
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{i}
consisting of non-redundant
x
’s.

(i)

There is a filtered Smith–Toda complex
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{j_{1}},\ldots,\phi_{j_{s}})
which lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i})
and
\mathsf{T}
.

In particular
\tau^{-1}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{j_{1}},\ldots,\phi_{j_{s}})
lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\tau^{-1}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i})
so has a vanishing line of slope
\tfrac{d_{i+1}}{n_{i+1}}
.

(ii)

\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i})
lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{j_{1}},\ldots,\phi_{j_{s}})
.

(iii)

For each
1\leq r\leq s
the endomorphism
\phi_{j_{r}}
of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{j_{1}},\ldots,\phi_{j_{r-1}})
is non-nilpotent after inverting
\tau
.

Proof.

The Smith–Toda complexes in the statements depend on the choices of self maps
\phi
, but the claims about them do not: using Lemma
2.5
(
iv
), Theorem
6.7
(
ii
) and Theorem
6.7
(
iii
) the thick subcategory generated by a
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{k})
is independent of the specific
\phi
’s used. We will not comment any further on this point.

For (
i
) we will build
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{j_{1}},\phi_{j_{2}},\ldots,\phi_{j_{r}})
inductively on
r
, showing that they lie in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\ldots,\phi_{j_{r+1}-1})
and
\mathsf{T}
. To start the induction, we show that
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\ldots,\phi_{j_{1}-1})
and
\mathsf{T}
. This is because
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots x_{j_{1}-1}
are redundant, so the
\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\ldots\phi_{j_{1}-1}
are nilpotent up to
\tau
-power torsion, and hence the claim holds by iterated application of Lemma
6.8
.

Suppose for the inductive step that
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{j_{1}},\phi_{j_{2}},\ldots,\phi_{j_{r-1}})
exists and lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\ldots,\phi_{j_{r}-1})
and
\mathsf{T}
. In particular
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{j_{1}},\phi_{j_{2}},\ldots,\phi_{j_{r-1}})
lies in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{d_{j_{r}}}{n_{j_{r}}},\tfrac{f_{j_{r}}}{d_{j_{r}}})
, so it has a
x_{j_{r}}
self-map
\phi_{j_{r}}
by Theorem
6.6
. By the asymptotic uniqueness of
x_{j_{r}}
self-maps as well as their compatibility with cofibre sequences, it follows that the mapping cone
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{j_{1}},\phi_{j_{2}},\ldots,\phi_{j_{r}})
of this map lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\ldots,\phi_{j_{r}})
and
\mathsf{T}
. As
x_{j_{r}+1},x_{j_{r}+2},\ldots,x_{j_{r+1}-1}
are redundant an iterated application of Lemma
6.8
implies that
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{j_{1}},\phi_{j_{2}},\ldots,\phi_{j_{r}})
also lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\ldots,\phi_{j_{r+1}-1})
and
\mathsf{T}
, as required.

For (
ii
) note that by (
i
) and Lemma
6.9
(
ii
) the object
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{j_{1}},\phi_{j_{2}},\ldots,\phi_{j_{s}})
lies in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{d_{i+1}}{n_{i+1}},\tfrac{f_{i+1}}{d_{i+1}})
, and hence it admits
x_{j}
self-maps for all
1\leq j\leq i
by Theorem
6.6
. We may therefore construct a
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\ldots,\phi_{i})
from
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{j_{1}},\phi_{j_{2}},\ldots,\phi_{j_{s}})
by killing
x_{j}
self-maps for each
j\in\{1,2,\ldots,i\}\setminus\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{r}\}
.

For (
iii
), note that as
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\ldots,\phi_{j_{r}-1})
lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{j_{1}},\phi_{j_{2}},\ldots,\phi_{j_{r-1}})
and
\mathsf{T}
, if
(\tau\phi_{j_{r}})^{-1}
annihilated the latter then it would annihilate the former, and it does not because
x_{j_{r}}
is not redundant.
∎

6.4.
Changing coordinates

The discussion so far allows us to omit those
x_{i}
which lead to self-maps
\phi_{i}
of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1})
that are nilpotent after inverting
\tau
. This goes some way towards efficiency of Smith–Toda complexes, but is still not satisfactory as the following example indicates.

Example 6.13
.

With
\mathbbm{k}=\mathbb{F}_{2}
let
\mathbb{R}:=\mathbb{E}_{\infty}(S^{1,0}r\oplus S^{1,0}b)\cup^{E_{\infty}}_{r\cdot b}D^{2,1}\rho
. This has
Q^{E_{\infty}^{\text{nu}}}\mathbb{I}\simeq S^{1,0}r\oplus S^{1,0}b\oplus S^{2,1}\rho
, and so for the
x_{i}
’s (considered as in Section
5.4
to be associated to the canonical
E_{\infty}
-multiplicative filtration) we may take a sequence starting

r,b,Q^{1}(r),Q^{1}(b),\rho,\ldots.

We have
\pi_{*,0}(\mathbb{R})=\mathbb{F}_{2}[r,b]/(rb)
, so
r\cdot-
acts non-nilpotently here and so
x_{1}=r
is not redundant. But now
\pi_{*,0}(\mathbb{R}/r)=\mathbb{F}_{2}[r,b]/(rb,r)=\mathbb{F}_{2}[b]
and
b\cdot-
acts non-nilpotently here, so
x_{2}=b
is also not redundant.

However, if we take an alternative sequence
x_{i}^{\prime}
starting

r+b,b,Q^{1}(r),Q^{1}(b),\rho,\ldots

then
\pi_{*,0}(\mathbb{R}/(r+b))=\mathbb{F}_{2}[r,b]/(rb,r+b)=\mathbb{F}_{2}[b]/(b^{2})
and
b\cdot-
acts nilpotently here. In fact, using the spectral sequence for the canonical multiplicative filtration one may easily calculate that
\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{R}/(r+b))=0
for
d<\tfrac{1}{2}(n-1)
, so any
\mathbb{R}
-module endomorphism of
\mathbb{R}/(r+b)
of slope
<\tfrac{1}{2}
acts nilpotently: in particular
b\cdot-
does. So now
x^{\prime}_{2}=b
is redundant, and can proceed to
x^{\prime}_{3}
.

We wish to explain how to make systematic the kind of improvement pointed out in this example. This will include changing basis for the spaces
X_{N,D,F}\subset X_{*,*,*}\subset V_{*,*,*}
, as in this example, but also something more general than just changing basis. However, as we will explain in Example
6.16
at the end of this subsection, this is still not completely satisfactory from the point of view of efficiency of Smith–Toda complexes. In Section
9
we will explain a different method which, when it applies, allows us to produce truly efficient Smith–Toda complexes.

6.4.1.
Nilpotence of canonical self-maps

For fixed slopes
(\lambda,\mu)
, let
x_{i},\ldots,x_{j}
be the
x
’s of slopes
(\lambda,\mu)
, which are a homogeneous basis of

X_{\lambda,\mu}:=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}D/N=\lambda\\
F/D=\mu\end{subarray}}X_{N,D,F}.

By Theorem
6.6
a
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\in\mathsf{W}(\lambda,\mu)
admits central
x_{i},\ldots,x_{j}
self-maps
\phi_{i},\ldots,\phi_{j}
, and our goal in this section is to define and study a radical ideal

I_{\lambda,\mu}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})\subseteq\mathbbm{k}[x_{i},\ldots,x_{j}]=\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*}[X_{\lambda,\mu}]

which roughly speaking records the polynomials in
\phi_{i},\ldots,\phi_{j}
for which the induced self-map has an iterate which is
\tau
-power torsion. Importantly, we will see that this ideal does not depend on the choice of basis
x_{i},\ldots,x_{j}
of
X_{\lambda,\mu}
.

We define
I_{\lambda,\mu}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
as the kernel of a ring homomorphism

\chi:\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*}[X_{\lambda,\mu}]=\mathbbm{k}[x_{i},\ldots,x_{j}]\longrightarrow Z(\tau^{-1}\pi_{*,*,*}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})))_{\text{perf}},

whose target is the (colimit) perfection of the centre of the
\tau
-inverted endomorphism ring of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
. This is obtained by choosing
\phi_{i},\ldots,\phi_{j}\in Z(\pi_{*,*,*}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})))
with
C\tau\otimes\phi_{k}=u_{*}(x_{k}^{p^{M_{k}}})
, using Theorem
6.6
and Theorem
6.7
(
i
), using these to form the homomorphism

\chi^{\text{pre}}:\mathbbm{k}[x_{i}^{p^{M_{i}}},\ldots,x_{j}^{p^{M_{j}}}]\longrightarrow Z(\tau^{-1}\pi_{*,*,*}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})))

given by
x_{k}^{p^{M_{k}}}\mapsto\phi_{k}
, then taking perfections and precomposing with

x_{k}\mapsto(x_{k}^{p^{M_{k}}})^{1/p^{M_{k}}}:\mathbbm{k}[x_{i},\ldots,x_{j}]\longrightarrow\mathbbm{k}[x_{i}^{p^{M_{i}}},\ldots,x_{j}^{p^{M_{j}}}]_{\text{perf}}.

So concretely
\chi(x_{k}):=(\phi_{k})^{1/p^{M_{k}}}
. It is elementary to check using Theorem
6.7
that this definition of
\chi
does not depend on the choice of self-maps
\phi_{i},\ldots,\phi_{j}
, and—when its domain is viewed as the symmetric algebra on
X_{\lambda,\mu}
—does not depend on the choice of homogeneous basis
x_{i},\ldots,x_{j}
for
X_{\lambda,\mu}
.

Lemma 6.14
.

(i)

If
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
and
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N}
are in
\mathsf{W}(\lambda,\mu)
and
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N}
is in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
and
\mathsf{T}
, then

I_{\lambda,\mu}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})\subseteq I_{\lambda,\mu}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N}).

(ii)

If
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1})
and
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\phi^{\prime}_{i-1})
are two Smith–Toda complexes obtained by iteratedly killing a sequence of
x_{1},\ldots,x_{i-1}
self-maps, then
I_{\lambda,\mu}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1}))=I_{\lambda,\mu}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\phi^{\prime}_{i-1}))
. In view of this we write

I_{\lambda,\mu}:=I_{\lambda,\mu}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1})).

(iii)

If
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\in\mathsf{W}(\lambda,\mu)
then
I_{\lambda,\mu}\subseteq I_{\lambda,\mu}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
.

(iv)

If
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\in\mathsf{W}(\lambda,\mu)
and
x_{j+1}
has slopes
(\bar{\lambda},\bar{\mu})
, then
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\in\mathsf{W}(\bar{\lambda},\bar{\mu})
if and only if
I_{\lambda,\mu}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})=(x_{i},\ldots,x_{j})
.

Proof.

For (
i
), note that if
y\in I_{\lambda,\mu}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})
then
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\in\mathsf{N}(y)
, so by Lemma
6.11
it follows that
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N}\in\mathsf{N}(y)
too, i.e.
y\in I_{\lambda,\mu}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{N})
.

For (
ii
), by Lemma
6.9
each of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1})
and
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\phi^{\prime}_{i-1})
lies in the thick subcategory generated by the other and
\mathsf{T}
, so the claim follows by (
i
).

For (
iii
) we apply Lemma
6.9
and (
i
).

For the reverse direction of (
iv
) first observe that
\mathsf{W}(\bar{\lambda},\bar{\mu})
is generated as a thick subcategory by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{j})
and
\mathsf{T}
by Lemma
6.9
, so if
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\in\mathsf{W}(\bar{\lambda},\bar{\mu})
then by (
i
) we have

I_{\lambda,\mu}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{j}))\subseteq I_{\lambda,\mu}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}).

But it follows from Lemma
6.11
(
iii
) that
x_{i},\ldots,x_{j}\in I_{\lambda,\mu}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{j}))
, so
I_{\lambda,\mu}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})=(x_{i},\ldots,x_{j})
as required.

For the forwards direction of (
iv
) suppose that
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\in\mathsf{W}(\lambda,\mu)
and
I_{\lambda,\mu}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})=(x_{i},\ldots,x_{j})
. Firstly, the
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
-module
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{k-1})
is finite, so the objects
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{k-1})
all lie in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
, so in particular lie in
\mathsf{W}(\lambda,\mu)
. Thus for
1\leq k\leq i-1
the endomorphism
\mathrm{Id}\otimes\phi_{k}
of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{k-1})
has an iterate which is
\tau
-power torsion. By Lemma
6.8
it follows that
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1})
and
\mathsf{T}
. Secondly, as
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1})
lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
, it follows from (
i
) that

(x_{i},\ldots,x_{j})=I_{N,D,F}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M})\subseteq I_{N,D,F}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1})),

i.e. for
i\leq k\leq j
the endomorphism
\mathrm{Id}\otimes\phi_{k}
of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{k-1})
has an iterate which is
\tau
-power torsion. By Lemma
6.8
it follows that the object
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i-1})
lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{j})
and
\mathsf{T}
. Thirdly, as
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
is finite it follows that
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{j})
lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{j})
, so in particular lies in
\mathsf{W}(\bar{\lambda},\bar{\mu})
. Putting this all together, it follows that
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{M}
lies in
\mathsf{W}(\bar{\lambda},\bar{\mu})
.
∎

6.4.2.
The elements
y_{i}

In view of the discussion in the previous section, we may improve upon the list of elements
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots
. Namely, given slopes
(\lambda,\mu)
we choose elements

y_{1}^{\lambda,\mu},\ldots,y^{\lambda,\mu}_{s_{\lambda,\mu}}\in\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*}[X_{\lambda,\mu}]

such that the composition

\mathbbm{k}[y_{1}^{\lambda,\mu},\ldots,y^{\lambda,\mu}_{s_{\lambda,\mu}}]\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*}[X_{\lambda,\mu}]\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*}[X_{\lambda,\mu}]/I_{\lambda,\mu}

is a Noether normalisation (i.e. is injective and the target is module-finite over the source). The number
s_{\lambda,\mu}
is the Krull dimension of
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{*}[X_{\lambda,\mu}]/I_{\lambda,\mu}
, which by the discussion in the previous section is canonically associated to
\mathbb{R}
. The elements
y_{1}^{\lambda,\mu},\ldots,y^{\lambda,\mu}_{s_{\lambda,\mu}}
will serve as a replacement for the basis
x_{i},\ldots,x_{j}
of
X_{\lambda,\mu}
. There may be fewer of them: this is to be considered an advantage.

Recall that the triply-graded vector space
X_{*,*,*}
had components
X_{N,D,F}
which we ordered by
D/N
-slope and then by
F/D
-slope, i.e.

X_{1,0,-1};X_{2,1,-2},X_{2,1,-1};X_{3,2,-3},X_{3,2,-2},X_{3,2,-1};X_{4,3,-4},\ldots

We let
y_{1},y_{2},\ldots
be the sequence obtained by concatenating the
y_{1}^{\lambda,\mu},\ldots,y^{\lambda,\mu}_{s_{\lambda,\mu}}
in this order. Let the tridegree of
y_{i}
be denoted
(N_{i},D_{i},F_{i})
.

Theorem 6.15
.

Let
\lambda<1
be given, and let
y_{1},\ldots,y_{r}
be those
y
’s with
\tfrac{D_{i}}{N_{i}}<\lambda
. Then there is a sequence of endomorphisms

\psi_{s}:S^{p^{M_{s}}N_{s},p^{M_{s}}D_{s},p^{M_{s}}F_{s}}\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{s-1})\longrightarrow\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{s-1})

for
s=1,\ldots,r
such that:

(i)

\psi_{s}
is a
y_{s}
self-map.

(ii)

\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{s-1})
lies in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D_{s}}{N_{s}},\tfrac{F_{s}}{D_{s}})
for all
0\leq s-1\leq r
.

(iii)

\pi_{*,*}(\tau^{-1}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{r}))
has a vanishing line of slope
\tfrac{D_{r+1}}{N_{r+1}}
and so in particular of slope
\lambda
.

(iv)

If
M
is pre-specified then the
\psi_{i}
may be chosen so that
M_{i}\geq M
for all
i
.

Proof.

Observe that (
iii
) follows from (
ii
) by setting
r-1=s
and inverting
\tau
.

We will make a sequence
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{s})
for all
s
, satisfying (
i
), (
ii
), and (
iv
). We do this inductively, so suppose for the inductive step that
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{s-1})
exists satisfying (
i
), (
ii
), and (
iv
). By (
ii
) it lies in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D_{s}}{N_{s}},\tfrac{F_{s}}{D_{s}})
, so by Theorem
6.6
it admits a
y_{s}
self-map
\psi_{s}
. By raising it to a further
p
-th power, we may suppose that
M_{s}\geq M
as required by (
iv
). Using this we may form
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{s})
, which satisfies (
i
) by definition, and lies in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D_{s}}{N_{s}},\tfrac{F_{s}}{D_{s}})
as this is a thick subcategory.

If
\tfrac{D_{s}}{N_{s}}=\tfrac{D_{s+1}}{D_{s+1}}
and
\tfrac{F_{s}}{D_{s}}=\tfrac{F_{s+1}}{D_{s+1}}
then this means that (
ii
) is satisfied. If not, then the sequence
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
is the union of sequences
y^{1}_{\lambda,\mu},\ldots,y_{\lambda,\mu}^{s_{\lambda,\mu}}
for all
(\lambda,\mu)<(\tfrac{D_{s+1}}{N_{s+1}},\frac{F_{s+1}}{D_{s+1}})
. Let
x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}
be the
x
’s with slopes
<(\tfrac{D_{s+1}}{N_{s+1}},\frac{F_{s+1}}{D_{s+1}})
, and consider

\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{s})\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i}).

The second factor is in the thick subcategory
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D_{s+1}}{N_{s+1}},\tfrac{F_{s+1}}{D_{s+1}})
by Lemma
6.9
(
i
), so the whole object is too.

Claim.
For each
1\leq k\leq i
the endomorphism
\mathrm{Id}\otimes\phi_{k}
of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{s})\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{k-1})
has an iterate which is
\tau
-power torsion.

Proof of Claim.

There are two cases. If
x_{k}
has slopes
<(\tfrac{D_{s}}{N_{s}},\tfrac{F_{s}}{D_{s}})
, then because
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{s})\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{k-1})\in\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D_{s}}{N_{s}},\tfrac{F_{s}}{D_{s}})
(as the first factor is), it immediately follows that some iterate of
\mathrm{Id}\otimes\phi_{k}
is
\tau
-power torsion. So consider those
x_{j},\ldots,x_{i}
which have slopes
(\tfrac{D_{s}}{N_{s}},\tfrac{F_{s}}{D_{s}})
, and let
y_{t},\ldots,y_{s}
be the
y
’s of the same slopes. We make two observations about the ideal
I_{D_{s}/N_{s},F_{s}/D_{s}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{s}))
:

(i)

y_{t},\ldots,y_{s}\in I_{D_{s}/N_{s},F_{s}/D_{s}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{r}))
by Lemma
6.11
(
iii
).

(ii)

By Lemma
6.14
(
iv
),
I_{D_{s}/N_{s},F_{s}/D_{s}}\subseteq I_{D_{s}/N_{s},F_{s}/D_{s}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{r}))
.

As
y_{t},\ldots,y_{s}
was by definition chosen to be a Noether normalisation for the algebra
\mathbbm{k}[x_{j},\ldots,x_{i}]/I_{D_{s}/N_{s},F_{s}/D_{s}}
, and
I_{D_{s}/N_{s},F_{s}/D_{s}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{s}))
is a radical ideal, it follows that
I_{D_{s}/N_{s},F_{s}/D_{s}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{s}))=(x_{j},\ldots,x_{i})
. By Lemma
6.14
(
i
) it follows that

I_{D_{s}/N_{s},F_{s}/D_{s}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{s})\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{j-1}))=(x_{j},\ldots,x_{i})

too, so the endomorphisms
\phi_{j},\ldots,\phi_{i}
have iterates which are
\tau
-power torsion too.
∎

Using this claim, several applications of Lemma
6.8
gives that
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{s})
lies in the thick subcategory generated by
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{s})\otimes_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i})
and
\mathsf{T}
, so lies in
\mathsf{W}(\tfrac{D_{s+1}}{N_{s+1}},\tfrac{F_{s+1}}{D_{s+1}})
, which finishes the proof of (
ii
).
∎

In the sequence of endomorphisms provided by Theorem
6.15
, it may be that a
\psi_{s}
is nilpotent up to
\tau
-power torsion as an endomorphism of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{s-1})
. By the argument given in Section
6.3
, these may be omitted.

6.5.
Scope for further efficiency

The following example indicates that the discussion so far is still not completely satisfactory from the point of view of efficiency of Smith–Toda complexes.

Example 6.16
.

With
\mathbbm{k}=\mathbb{F}_{2}
let
\mathbb{R}:=\mathbb{E}_{\infty}(S^{1,0}\sigma\oplus S^{2,1}x)\cup^{E_{\infty}}_{x\cdot Q^{1}(\sigma)}D^{4,3}\rho
, and
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}
denote its canonical
E_{\infty}
-multiplicative filtration. Write
\hat{\sigma}\in\pi_{1,0,-1}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R})
and
\hat{x}\in\pi_{2,1,-1}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R})
for filtered representatives of
\sigma
and
x
of (minimal) filtration
-1
, and write
\sigma^{\prime},x^{\prime}\in\pi_{*,*,*}(C\tau\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R})
for their reductions modulo
\tau
. For the
x_{i}
’s we may take a sequence starting

\sigma^{\prime},Q^{1}(\sigma^{\prime}),x^{\prime},Q^{2}Q^{1}(\sigma^{\prime}),Q^{2}(x^{\prime}),\ldots\in\pi_{*,*,*}(C\tau\otimes\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}).

Certainly
\sigma\cdot-
is non-nilpotent on
\mathbb{R}
, and we must form
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/\hat{\sigma}
. Now
Q^{1}(\sigma^{\prime})
has tridegree
(2,1,-2)
and
x^{\prime}
has tridegree
(2,1,-1)
, and these are the only
x_{i}
’s of
\tfrac{d}{n}
-slope
\tfrac{1}{2}
. As they have different tridegrees they cannot be considered together and must be considered in increasing order of
\tfrac{f}{d}
-slope.

We must first construct a
Q^{1}(\sigma^{\prime})
self-map of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/\hat{\sigma}
, which we may take to be the filtered map
Q^{1}(\hat{\sigma})\cdot-
as
\hat{\sigma}
lifts
\sigma^{\prime}
. By base-change along the
E_{\infty}
-map
\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{E}_{\infty}(S^{1,0}\sigma)
killing
x
and
\rho
, we see that
Q^{1}(\sigma)\cdot-
is non-nilpotent on
\mathbb{R}/\sigma
.

We now construct a
x^{\prime}
self-map of
\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/(\hat{\sigma},Q^{1}(\hat{\sigma}))
, which can be taken to be
\hat{x}\cdot-
because
\hat{x}
lifts
x^{\prime}
. By base-change along the
E_{\infty}
-map
\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{E}_{\infty}(S^{2,1}x)
killing
\sigma
and
\rho
, one sees that
x\cdot-
is non-nilpotent on
\mathbb{R}/(\sigma,Q^{1}(\sigma))
. Then
\mathbb{R}/(\sigma,Q^{1}(\sigma),x)
has a slope
\tfrac{1}{2}
vanishing line.

But this example is highly similar to Example
6.13
, in that
\mathbb{R}/(\sigma,Q^{1}(\sigma)+x)
already has a slope
\tfrac{1}{2}
vanishing line. This is because
(Q^{1}(\sigma)+x)\cdot-
is nilpotent on
\mathbb{R}/(\sigma,Q^{1}(\sigma)+x)
, but also
x\cdot Q^{1}(\sigma)=0\in\pi_{*,*}(\mathbb{R})
, and together these show that
Q^{1}(\sigma)\cdot-
and
x\cdot-
are both nilpotent on
\mathbb{R}/(\sigma,Q^{1}(\sigma)+x)
.

However the slope
\frac{1}{2}
Smith–Toda complex
\mathbb{R}/(\sigma,Q^{1}(\sigma)+x)
can never arise from the mechanism we have discussed so far: the mechanism obliges us to kill
Q^{1}(\sigma^{\prime})\cdot-=(C\tau\otimes u)_{*}(Q^{1}(\sigma^{\prime}))
first because it has lower
\tfrac{f}{d}
-slope than
x^{\prime}
and is non-nilpotent, so if we do not kill it then we will not generally know that the element
(C\tau\otimes u)_{*}(x^{\prime})
of higher
\tfrac{f}{d}
-slope is a permanent cycle in the spectral sequence for
\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/\hat{\sigma})
. But in this example we see that
(C\tau\otimes u)_{*}(x^{\prime})
is indeed a permanent cycle in this spectral sequence as
u_{*}(\hat{x})
is a filtered homotopy class lifting it, and furthermore we see that choosing the lift
u_{*}(\hat{x}+Q^{1}(\hat{\sigma}))\in\pi_{2,1,-1}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/\hat{\sigma}))
to a filtered homotopy class is wiser than choosing the lift
u_{*}(\hat{x})\in\pi_{2,1,-1}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}}(\mathrm{fil}_{*}\mathbb{R}/\hat{\sigma}))
.

7.
Changing rings and detecting nilpotence

Which
x_{i}
can be omitted, the ideals
I_{\lambda,\mu}
, and which
y_{i}
may be omitted, are all described in terms of nilpotence of endomorphisms up to
\tau
-power torsion, or equivalently in terms of nilpotence after inverting
\tau
. To make working with these concepts effective, we need tools for testing the (non-)nilpotence of endomorphisms of
\mathbb{R}
-modules. The following gives us a way to check nilpotence after base-changing along a suitable map
\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{S}
, which can be used to reduce the question to working over a simpler
E_{k}
-algebra. We will explain in Section
8
that there is a very good choice of
\mathbb{S}
to which we can apply this theorem.

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
\mathbbm{k}
be a commutative ring,
\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{S}
be a morphism in
\mathsf{Alg}_{E_{2}}(\mathsf{D}(\mathbbm{k})^{\mathbb{Z}})
between objects satisfying (C), and suppose that
\theta\geq 0
is such that

(
\dagger
)

H_{n,d}^{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{S})\text{ is just $\mathbbm{k}[0,0]$ in the range of bidegrees $d<\theta n+1$ or $n<1$}.

(i)

Let
\mathbb{M}
be an
\mathbb{R}
-module,
\lambda\geq 0
and
\kappa
be given, and
N
be such that
\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{M})=0
for
n<N
. Suppose that
\theta\geq\lambda
. Then

\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{M})=0\text{ for }d<\lambda n+\kappa\iff\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M})=0\text{ for }d<\lambda n+\kappa.

If these (equivalent) conditions hold, then the map

\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{M})\longrightarrow\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M})

is an epimorphism for
d<\lambda n+\kappa+(\theta-\lambda)+1
, and an isomorphism for
d<\lambda n+\kappa+(\theta-\lambda)
.

(ii)

Let
\mathbb{E}
be a unital associative algebra in (the homotopy category of)
\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathsf{mod}
and
\lambda\geq 0
,
\kappa
, and
N
be such that
\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{E})=0
for
d<\lambda n+\kappa
or for
n<N
. Suppose that
\theta>\lambda
. Then for
\tfrac{d}{n}=\lambda
elements in the kernel of

\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{E})\longrightarrow\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E})

are nilpotent.

(iii)

In the setting of (
ii
), suppose also that
\mathbbm{k}
is a field of positive characteristic
p
. If
x\in\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{S})
has slope
\tfrac{d}{n}=\lambda
then some
p
-th power of
(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}u)_{*}(x)\in\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E})
lifts to a
\psi\in\pi_{*,*}(\mathbb{E})
.

(iv)

In the setting of (
iii
), if
\mathbb{E}
is in addition a finite
\mathbb{R}
-module then some
p
-th power of the lift
\psi
lies in the centre of
\pi_{*,*}(\mathbb{E})
, and any two lifts
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
agree after taking further
p
-th powers.

We will prove this theorem by using the
\mathbb{S}
-based Adams spectral sequence in the category of
\mathbb{R}
-modules, so before giving the proof we begin with a general discussion of this spectral sequence.

We choose to construct this spectral sequence using the “canonical” Adams resolution, as follows. Let
\overline{\mathbb{S}}
denote the fibre of
\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{S}
, an
\mathbb{R}
-module, and set

\displaystyle F_{s}\mathbb{M}

\displaystyle:=\overline{\mathbb{S}}^{\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}s}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M}

\displaystyle K_{s}\mathbb{M}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{\mathbb{S}}^{\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}s}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M}

to obtain the tower

(7.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{0}\mathbb{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}\mathbb{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2}\mathbb{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}\mathbb{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{1}\mathbb{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{2}\mathbb{M}}

Taking homotopy groups yields an exact couple having

E_{1}^{n,s,t}:=\pi_{n,t-s}(K_{s}\mathbb{M}).

This exact couple gives a spectral sequence

(7.2)

E_{1}^{n,s,t}(\mathbb{M})\Longrightarrow\pi_{n,t-s}(\mathbb{M}),

with differentials
d_{r}:E_{r}^{n,s,t}(\mathbb{M})\to E_{r}^{n,s+r,t+r-1}(\mathbb{M})
.

There are pairings
E_{r}^{n,s,t}(\mathbb{M})\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}E_{r}^{n^{\prime},s^{\prime},t^{\prime}}(\mathbb{N})\to E_{r}^{n+n^{\prime},s+s^{\prime},t+t^{\prime}}(\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{N})
of spectral sequences converging to the tensor product map
\pi_{n,t-s}(\mathbb{M})\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\pi_{n^{\prime},t^{\prime}-s^{\prime}}(\mathbb{N})\to\pi_{n+n^{\prime},t+t^{\prime}-s-s^{\prime}}(\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{N})
. This is established precisely as for the classical Adams spectral sequence, e.g.
[
1
, §4]
. In particular, if
\mathbb{E}
is equipped with a product
\mu:\mathbb{E}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}\to\mathbb{E}
then it induces a product on
\{E_{r}^{*,*,*}(\mathbb{E})\}_{r}
, enjoying the same unitality or associativity properties as
\mathbb{E}
.

Strong convergence is guaranteed in our setting by the following (which can certainly be improved upon, but suffices for our kinds of applications).

Lemma 7.2
.

If
(\dagger)
holds (for any
\theta\geq 0
) and there is an
N
such that
\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{M})=0
for
n<N
then this spectral sequence converges strongly.

Proof.

We refer to Boardman
[
7
]
: in the language of that paper, this is a half-plane spectral sequence with entering differentials. We will use
[
7
, Theorem 7.3]
. We will first show that
\pi_{n,d}(F_{s}\mathbb{M})=0
for all
s\gg 0
, implying that the spectral sequence is conditionally convergent. We will then show that
E_{1}^{n,s,d+s}(\mathbb{M})=\pi_{n,d}(K_{s}\mathbb{M})=0
for all
s\gg 0
, implying that there are only finitely-many differentials out of each position, and hence that
RE_{\infty}=0
. By
[
7
, Theorem 7.3]
the spectral sequence converges strongly.

From
(\dagger
) we see that
H^{\mathbb{R}}_{0,d}(\mathbb{S})
is just
\mathbbm{k}
for
d=0
, and
H^{\mathbb{R}}_{n,0}(\mathbb{S})
is just
\mathbbm{k}
for
n=0
. From this it follows that
\mathbb{S}
can be constructed as an
\mathbb{R}
-module from
\mathbb{R}
by attaching
(n,d)
-
\mathbb{R}
-cells with
n>0
and
d>0
. Taking the skeletal filtration of such a model, we have associated graded

\mathrm{gr}(\mathbb{S})\simeq\mathbb{R}\oplus\bigoplus_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha},d_{\alpha}}\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\mathbb{R}

where
n_{\alpha}>0
and
d_{\alpha}>0
. It gives an associated filtration of
\overline{\mathbb{S}}
with
\mathrm{gr}(\overline{\mathbb{S}})\simeq\bigoplus_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha},d_{\alpha}-1}\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\mathbb{R}
, and hence a filtration of
F_{s}\mathbb{M}
with

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}(F_{s}\mathbb{M})\simeq(\bigoplus_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha},d_{\alpha}-1}\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\mathbb{R})^{\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}s}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M}\simeq(\bigoplus_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha},d_{\alpha}-1})^{\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}s}\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\mathbb{M}.

As
n_{\alpha}>0
and
d_{\alpha}-1\geq 0
,
(\bigoplus_{\alpha}S^{n_{\alpha},d_{\alpha}-1})^{\otimes s}
is connective and is supported in gradings
\geq s
. Under the given assumption on
\mathbb{M}
it follows that
\pi_{n,d}(\mathrm{gr}(F_{s}\mathbb{M}))=0
for
n<N+s
, so by strong convergence of the skeletal spectral sequence that
\pi_{n,d}(F_{s}\mathbb{M})=0
for
n<N+s
, as required.

The analogous filtration for
K_{s}\mathbb{M}
shows that
E_{1}^{n,s,d+s}(\mathbb{M})=\pi_{n,d}(K_{s}\mathbb{M})=0
for
n<N+s
, showing that
RE_{\infty}=0
as required.
∎

Before starting on the proof of Theorem
7.1
, we have the following.

Lemma 7.3
.

If
(\dagger)
holds then
\pi_{n,d}(\overline{\mathbb{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k})=0
for
n<1
or
d<\theta n
.

Proof.

We use the cofibre sequence

\overline{\mathbb{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k}(\simeq\mathbbm{k})\longrightarrow\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k}

and its long exact sequence

\cdots\pi_{n,d+1}(\mathbbm{k})\longrightarrow\pi_{n,d+1}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k})\longrightarrow\pi_{n,d}(\overline{\mathbb{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k})\longrightarrow\pi_{n,d}(\mathbbm{k})\longrightarrow\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k})\cdots,

in which
\pi_{0,0}(\mathbbm{k})=\mathbbm{k}
cancels against
\pi_{0,0}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k})=\mathbbm{k}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7.1
.

For the forwards implication of (
i
), we consider
\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M}
and to begin with we Postnikov filter
\mathbb{M}
, giving a filtered object
\tau_{\geq-*}\mathbb{M}
. As
\mathbb{R}
is connective, this is a filtration by
\mathbb{R}
-modules. Its associated graded is
\mathrm{gr}(\tau_{\geq-*}\mathbb{M})=\bigoplus_{d\in\mathbb{Z}}\Sigma^{d}\pi_{*,d}(\mathbb{M})
, where the
d
-th summand is in grading
-d
. This filtration induces a filtration of
\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M}
having

\mathrm{gr}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\tau_{\geq-*}\mathbb{M})=\bigoplus_{d\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\Sigma^{d}\pi_{*,d}(\mathbb{M}).

Here
\pi_{*,d}(\mathbb{M})
has the structure of an
\mathbb{R}
-module via the truncation
\mathbb{R}\to\tau_{\leq 0}\mathbb{R}=\pi_{*,0}(\mathbb{R})
, so we may write the above as

(7.3)

\bigoplus_{d\in\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\pi_{*,0}(\mathbb{R}))\otimes_{\pi_{*,0}(\mathbb{R})}\Sigma^{d}\pi_{*,d}(\mathbb{M}).

We now filter each
\pi_{*,d}(\mathbb{M})
by its grading, i.e.

\mathrm{fil}_{f}\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{M})=\begin{cases}\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{M})&f\geq n\\
0&f<n.\end{cases}

As
\pi_{n,0}(\mathbb{R})=0
for
n<0
, this is a filtration by
\pi_{*,0}(\mathbb{R})
-modules. The associated graded
\mathrm{gr}(\mathrm{fil}_{f}\pi_{*,d}(\mathbb{M}))
can be identified with
\pi_{*,d}(\mathbb{M})
, but the
\pi_{*,0}(\mathbb{R})
-module structure now factors over the augmentation
\epsilon:\pi_{*,0}(\mathbb{R})\to\pi_{0,0}(\mathbb{R})=\mathbbm{k}
. Taking the induced filtration on each summand of (
7.3
), the associated graded may be expressed by the same formula, but now the
\pi_{*,0}(\mathbb{R})
-module structure on
\Sigma^{d}\pi_{*,d}(\mathbb{M})
is via the augmentation. The formula can therefore be manipulated into

\bigoplus_{d\in\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\pi_{*,0}(\mathbb{R}))\otimes_{\pi_{*,0}(\mathbb{R})}\mathbbm{k}\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\Sigma^{d}\pi_{*,d}(\mathbb{M})\simeq\bigoplus_{d\in\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k})\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\Sigma^{d}\pi_{*,d}(\mathbb{M}).

Using the Künneth spectral sequence to calculate the homotopy groups of the latter, and neglecting several internal gradings, we therefore have a chain of (strongly convergent) spectral sequences

E^{2}_{n,p,q}=\mathrm{Tor}^{\mathbbm{k}}_{p}(\pi_{*,*}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k}),\pi_{*,*}(\mathbb{M}))_{n,q}\Longrightarrow\cdots\Longrightarrow\pi_{n,p+q}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M}).

Assumption (
\dagger
) is precisely that
\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k})=0
for
n<1
or
d<\theta n+1
except that it is
\mathbbm{k}
for
(n,d)=(0,0)
. Using the assumption
\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{M})=0
for
d<\lambda n+\kappa
too, it follows that

E^{2}_{n,p,q}=0\text{ for }q<\min(\lambda n+\kappa,\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a\geq 1\end{subarray}}(\theta a+1+\lambda b+\kappa),

so in particular for
p+q<\lambda n+\kappa
using that
\theta\geq\lambda
and
p\geq 0
. Running these spectral sequences shows that
\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M})=0
for
d<\lambda n+\kappa
as required.

For the reverse implication of (
i
), suppose that
\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M})=0
for
d<\lambda n+\kappa
. We can write

K_{s}\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{\mathbb{S}}^{\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}s}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M}=(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{\mathbb{S}}^{\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}s})\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M}).

We apply the same two filtrations as above to
\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M}
: first Postnikov filter it, then filter it by grading. These induce iterated filtrations of
K_{s}\mathbb{M}
, whose final associated graded is

\displaystyle((\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{\mathbb{S}}^{\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}s})\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}\mathbbm{k})\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathbb{Z}}\Sigma^{d}\pi_{*,d}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\simeq(\overline{\mathbb{S}}^{\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}s}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k})\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathbb{Z}}\Sigma^{d}\pi_{*,d}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\simeq(\overline{\mathbb{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k})^{\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}s}\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathbb{Z}}\Sigma^{d}\pi_{*,d}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M})\right).

(For the first equivalence we have used that
\mathbb{S}
is central in
\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathsf{mod}
, cf. Lemma
2.1
.) Using the Künneth spectral sequence to calculate the homotopy groups of the latter, and neglecting several internal gradings, we therefore have a chain of (strongly convergent) spectral sequences

E^{2}_{n,p,q}=\mathrm{Tor}^{\mathbbm{k}}_{p}(\pi_{*,*}((\overline{\mathbb{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k})^{\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}s}),\pi_{*,*}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M}))_{n,q}\Longrightarrow\cdots\Longrightarrow\pi_{n,p+q}(K_{s}\mathbb{M}).

By Lemma
7.3
we have
\pi_{n,d}(\overline{\mathbb{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k})=0
for
n<1
or
d<\theta n
, so running several Künneth spectral sequences it follows that
\pi_{n,d}((\overline{\mathbb{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k})^{\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}s})=0
for
n<s
or
d<\theta n
. Using the assumption
\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M})=0
for
d<\lambda n+\kappa
too, it follows that

E^{2}_{n,p,q}=0\text{ for }q<\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b=n\\
a\geq s\end{subarray}}(\theta a+\lambda b+\kappa),

so in particular for
p+q<\lambda n+\kappa+s(\theta-\lambda)
, using that
\theta\geq\lambda
and
p\geq 0
. Running these spectral sequences shows that
\pi_{n,d}(K_{s}\mathbb{M})=0
for
d<\lambda n+\kappa+s(\theta-\lambda)
. By our assumption that
\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{M})=0
for
n<N
, Lemma
7.2
shows that the Adams spectral sequence converges strongly, so running it shows that
\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{M})=0
for
d<\lambda n+\kappa
as required.

For the addendum to (
i
), note that the map
\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{M})\to\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{M})=\pi_{n,d}(K_{0}\mathbb{M})=E_{1}^{n,0,d}(\mathbb{M})
is the edge homomorphism of the Adams spectral sequence. By strong convergence, for it to be an epimorphism it suffices that all possible targets
E_{r}^{n,r,d+r-1}(\mathbb{M})
of differentials out of
E_{r}^{n,0,d}(\mathbb{M})
vanish, and for this it suffices for
E_{1}^{n,r,d+r-1}(\mathbb{M})=\pi_{n,d-1}(K_{r}\mathbb{M})
to vanish for all
r\geq 1
, and the estimate above shows this happens as long as
d-1<\lambda n+\kappa+(\theta-\lambda)
. For it to be a monomorphism means to have
E_{\infty}^{n,s,d+s}(\mathbb{M})=0
for all
s\geq 1
, and for this it suffices to have
E_{1}^{n,s,d+s}(\mathbb{M})=\pi_{n,d}(K_{s}\mathbb{M})=0
for all
s\geq 1
, and the estimate above shows this happens as long as
d<\lambda n+\kappa+(\theta-\lambda)
.

For (
ii
), we consider the Adams spectral sequence

E_{1}^{n,s,t}(\mathbb{E})\Longrightarrow\pi_{n,t-s}(\mathbb{E}),

which is equipped with a unital and associative multiplicative structure as
\mathbb{E}
is an
E_{1}
-algebra in
\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathsf{mod}
. By our assumption that
\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{E})=0
for
n<N
, Lemma
7.2
shows that it converges strongly. We have
E_{1}^{n,0,d}(\mathbb{E})=\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E})
, and the edge homomorphism

\Phi:\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{E})\longrightarrow E_{1}^{n,0,d}(\mathbb{E})=\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E})

is the canonical map. Our assumption is that a class
\Phi(x)\in E_{1}^{n,0,d}(\mathbb{E})
is nilpotent: after replacing
x
by a power, we may assume that
\Phi(x)=0\in E_{1}^{n,0,d}(\mathbb{E})
. That is, the class
x\in\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{E})
has
\mathbb{S}
-based Adams filtration
\geq 1
. To conclude the argument, we will show:

Claim.
For each
\tilde{\kappa}\in\mathbb{Z}
there is an
s_{0}
such that
E_{1}^{\ell n,s,\ell d+\tilde{\kappa}+s}(\mathbb{E})=0
for all
s\geq s_{0}
and all
\ell
.

It follows from this Claim with
\tilde{\kappa}=0
, and strong convergence of the spectral sequence, that a class in
\pi_{\ell n,\ell d}(\mathbb{E})
of
\mathbb{S}
-Adams filtration
\geq s_{0}
is zero. As
x\in\pi_{n,d}(\mathbb{E})
has
\mathbb{S}
-Adams filtration
\geq 1
, the class
x^{\ell}\in\pi_{\ell n,\ell d}(\mathbb{E})
has
\mathbb{S}
-Adams filtration
\geq\ell
, so as long as
\ell\geq s_{0}
we have
x^{\ell}=0
. Thus
x
is nilpotent as required.

Proof of Claim.

By definition
E_{1}^{\ell n,s,\ell d+\tilde{\kappa}+s}(\mathbb{E})=\pi_{\ell n,\ell d+\tilde{\kappa}}(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{\mathbb{S}}^{\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}s}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E})
. Filtering
\mathbb{E}
by its grading induces a filtration of
\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{\mathbb{S}}^{\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}s}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}
with associated graded
(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{\mathbb{S}}^{\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}s}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k})\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\mathbb{E}
. As
-\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{k}:\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathsf{mod}\to\mathbbm{k}\text{-}\mathsf{mod}
